    <h2 class="tab-header-label">Factsheet</h2>
</div><div class="tab-content"><div class="tab-content-body"><section class="factsheet"><div class="factsheet-left"><section id="factsheet-meaning" class="factsheet-section"><h3 class="factsheet-section-header">          What does the verb <i>fleet</i> mean?        </h3><div class="factsheet-meaning-text"><p>There are <b>20</b> meanings listed in OED's entry for the verb <i>fleet</i>, 12 of which are labelled obsolete. See &lsquo;Meaning &amp; use&rsquo; for definitions, usage, and quotation evidence.</p></div><div class="factsheet-topics"><p><i>fleet</i> has developed          meanings and uses in subjects including        </p><div class="tags"><a class="tag subject-tag factsheet-subject-tag" href="/dictionary/fleet_v1?tab=meaning_and_use#4071892" data-target-element="4071892">nautical (Old English)            </a><a class="tag subject-tag factsheet-subject-tag" href="/dictionary/fleet_v1?tab=meaning_and_use#4072034" data-target-element="4072034">fish (Old English)            </a></div></div><div class="factsheet-entry-status-note"><h4>Entry status</h4><p>            OED is undergoing a continuous programme of revision to modernize and improve definitions.            This entry has not yet been fully revised.          </p></div><div class="section-footer"><a class="call-to-action factsheet-onward-link" href="/dictionary/fleet_v1?tab=meaning_and_use" data-target-tab="meaning_and_use">            See meaning &amp; use</a></div></section><section id="factsheet-frequency" class="factsheet-section"><h3 class="factsheet-section-header">          How common is the verb <i>fleet</i>?        </h3><p><i>fleet</i> is a word inherited from <b>Germanic</b>.</p><div class="section-footer"><a class="call-to-action factsheet-onward-link" href="/dictionary/fleet_v1?tab=etymology" data-target-tab="etymology">            See etymology</a></div></section><section id="factsheet-earliest_use" class="factsheet-section"><h3 class="factsheet-section-header">          What is the earliest known use of the verb <i>fleet</i>?        </h3><div class="section-columns"><div class="callout-circle earliest-known-date"><p>Earliest known use</p><p>Old English</p></div><div class="earliest-known-use"><p>The earliest known use of the verb <i>fleet</i> is in the Old English period (pre-1150).</p><p>It is also recorded as a noun from the Old English period (pre-1150).</p></div></div><div class="section-footer"><a class="call-to-action factsheet-onward-link" href="/dictionary/fleet_v1?tab=meaning_and_use" data-target-tab="meaning_and_use">            See meaning &amp; use</a></div></section><section id="factsheet-pronunciation" class="factsheet-section"><h3 class="factsheet-section-header">          How is the verb <i>fleet</i> pronounced?     <h2 class="tab-header-label">Etymology</h2>
</div><div class="tab-content"><div class="tab-content-body"><div><div class="etymology-summary" id="etymology_summary"><h3 class="etymology-summary-header">Summary</h3><div>A word inherited from Germanic.</div></div><div id="main_etymology_truncated" class="etymology hidden">A Common <span class="language-name">Germanic</span> originally strong verb; <span class="language-name">Old English</span> <span class="foreign-form">fl&eacute;otan</span> (<span class="foreign-form">fl&eacute;at</span>, <span class="foreign-form">fluton</span>, <span class="foreign-form">floten</span>) to float, corresponding to <span class="language-name">Old Frisian</span> <span class="foreign-form">fliata</span>, <span class="language-name">Old Saxon</span> <span class="foreign-form">fliotan</span> (<span class="language-name">Middle Dutch</span>, <span class="language-name">Dutch</span> <span class="foreign-form">vlieten</span>) to flow&hellip;      &nbsp;&nbsp;      <button id="main_etymology_show_more" type="button" class="quotations-button">Show more</button></div><div id="main_etymology_complete" class="etymology">A Common <span class="language-name">Germanic</span> originally strong verb; <span class="language-name">Old English</span> <span class="foreign-form">fl&eacute;otan</span> (<span class="foreign-form">fl&eacute;at</span>, <span class="foreign-form">fluton</span>, <span class="foreign-form">floten</span>) to float, corresponding to <span class="language-name">Old Frisian</span> <span class="foreign-form">fliata</span>, <span class="language-name">Old Saxon</span> <span class="foreign-form">fliotan</span> (<span class="language-name">Middle Dutch</span>, <span class="language-name">Dutch</span> <span class="foreign-form">vlieten</span>) to flow, <span class="language-name">Old High German</span> <span class="foreign-form">flioȥȥan</span> to float, flow (<span class="language-name">Middle High German</span> <span class="foreign-form">vlieȥen</span>, modern <span class="language-name">German</span> <span class="foreign-form">flie&szlig;en</span> to flow), <span class="language-name">Old Norse</span> <span class="foreign-form">fli&oacute;ta</span> (<span class="language-name">Swedish</span> <span class="foreign-form">flyta</span>, <span class="language-name">Danish</span> <span class="foreign-form">fl&oslash;de</span>) to float, flow (not recorded in <span class="language-name">Gothic</span>) <span class="etymology-arrow">&lt;</span> <span class="language-name">Old Germanic</span> <span class="foreign-form">*fleutan</span> (<span class="foreign-form">flaut</span>, <span class="foreign-form">flutum</span>, <span class="foreign-form">flotono-</span>), <span class="etymology-arrow">&lt;</span> <span class="language-name">pre-Germanic</span> root <span class="foreign-form">*pleud-</span>, <span class="foreign-form">ploud-</span>, <span class="foreign-form">plud-</span> (compare <span class="language-name">Latvian</span> <span class="foreign-form">pludināt</span> to float, <span class="foreign-form">pludot</span> to flow, <span class="foreign-form">plūdi</span> flood, <span class="language-name">Lithuanian</span> <span class="foreign-form">pl&uacute;sti</span> to float away, <span class="foreign-form">plūdīs</span> float of a fishing-net), an extended form of the <span class="language-name">Old Aryan</span> root <span class="foreign-form">*pleu-</span>, <span class="foreign-form">plu-</span> (compare <span class="language-name">Greek</span> <span class="foreign-form">πλεῖν</span> to sail, <span class="language-name">Sanskrit</span> <span class="foreign-form">plu</span>, <span class="foreign-form">pru</span> to swim, float, flow, <span class="language-name">Latin</span> <span class="foreign-form">pluĕre</span> to rain.<div><button id="main_etymology_show_less" type="button" class="hidden quotations-button">Show less</button></div></div><div><button class="modal-trigger cite-button" type="button" data-trigger-for="cite-modal" aria-controls="cite-modal" Also with&hellip;</div></a></li></ol></li><li class="toc-item treeview-item expandable "><button class="toc-expand-button expandable" type="button">Expand</button><a href="#4072509" data-target-element="4072509"><div class="toc-item-enumerator">II.10.</div><div class="toc-item-text">To glide away like a stream; to slip away, change position&hellip;</div></a><ol class="toc-list treeview-list hidden indented"><li class="toc-item treeview-item  "><a href="#4072510" data-target-element="4072510"><div class="toc-item-enumerator">II.10.a.</div><div class="toc-item-text">To glide away like a stream; to slip away, change position&hellip;</div></a></li><li class="toc-item treeview-item  "><a href="#4072599" data-target-element="4072599"><div class="toc-item-enumerator">II.10.b.</div><div class="toc-item-text">Of the soul: To pass away from the body; hence said of a&hellip;</div></a></li><li class="toc-item treeview-item  "><a href="#4072642" data-target-element="4072642"><div class="toc-item-enumerator">II.10.c.</div><div class="toc-item-text">Of time: To pass rapidly and imperceptibly; to slip away&hellip;</div></a></li><li class="toc-item treeview-item  "><a href="#4072697" data-target-element="4072697"><div class="toc-item-enumerator">II.10.d.</div><div class="toc-item-text">transitive. Also with&hellip;</div></a></li></ol></li><li class="toc-item treeview-item  "><a href="#4072791" data-target-element="4072791"><div class="toc-item-enumerator">III.</div><div class="toc-item-text">Nautical uses.</div></a><ol class="toc-list treeview-list shown indented"><li class="toc-item treeview-item  "><a href="#4072792" data-target-element="4072792"><div class="toc-item-enumerator">III.12.</div><div class="toc-item-text">Nautical. transitive. Also, <i>transferred</i> to drift <i>away</i>.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/pour_v?tab=meaning_and_use#29104647">pour</a></span><span class="daterange">1538&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>intransitive</i>. Frequently with&hellip;</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/slip_v1?tab=meaning_and_use#22252615">slip</a></span><span class="daterange">1596&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition">Of rivers, etc.: To run smoothly or gently; to flow, glide; to pass <i>into</i> the sea.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/streek_v?tab=meaning_and_use#20567877">streek</a></span><span class="daterange">1598&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>transferred</i>, e.g. of the sun in its course. Also: (of land)&hellip;</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/rindle_v?tab=meaning_and_use#25407220">rindle</a></span><span class="daterange">1863&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>intransitive</i>. Of a stream, etc.: to flow gently; to trickle.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/slosh_v1?tab=meaning_and_use#22286806">slosh</a></span><span class="daterange">a1953&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition">Of liquid: to splash; to flow in streams.</div></div></li></ol></div><div class="thesaurus-panel-link"><a class="call-to-action" href="/thesaurus/?classId=3981">View in Historical Thesaurus</a></div></div></div></div></div></li><li class="item sense subsense " id="4072313"><div class="item-content"><div class="item-enumerator">II.7.b.</div><div class="item-body"><div class="daterange-container"><span class="daterange">1596&ndash;1638</span></div> <div class="definition">&dagger;&nbsp;<span class="editorial-label">transferred</span>. Also <i>figurative</i>. <i>Scottish</i> in later use.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/fleet_v1?tab=meaning_and_use#4072382">fleet</a></span><span class="daterange">c1400</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>transitive</i>. In extended use: to spread through so as to prevail over or dominate, esp. in a harmful or injurious manner; to overwhelm or take&hellip;</div></div></li></ol></div><div class="thesaurus-panel-link"><a class="call-to-action" href="/thesaurus/?classId=97542&amp;openAt=97548">View in Historical Thesaurus</a></div></div><div class="thesaurus-category"><div class="thesaurus-breadcrumb"><a href="/thesaurus/?classId=1"><span class="thesaurus-breadcrumb-label">the world</span></a> <a href="/thesaurus/?classId=111290"><span class="thesaurus-breadcrumb-label">relative properties</span></a> <a href="/thesaurus/?classId=117847"><span class="thesaurus-breadcrumb-label">quantity</span></a> <a href="/thesaurus/?classId=118062"><span class="thesaurus-breadcrumb-label">sufficient quantity, amount, or degree</span></a> <a href="/thesaurus/?classId=118063"><span class="thesaurus-breadcrumb-label">abundance</span></a> <a href="/thesaurus/?classId=118111"><span class="thesaurus-breadcrumb-label">[transitive verbs] make abundant</span></a> <a href="/thesaurus/?classId=118111&amp;openAt=118115"><span class="thesaurus-breadcrumb-label">fill (a place) abundantly</span></a> </div><div class="thesaurus-instance-wrapper"><ol class="thesaurus-instance-list"><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/fleet_v1?tab=meaning_and_use#4072382">fleet</a></span><span class="daterange">c1400</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>transitive</i>. class="definition"><span class="grammar">intransitive</span>.</div></div></div><ol class="list s6-list"><li class="item sense subsense " id="4072398"><div class="item-content"><div Now <i>rare</i>.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/leer_v?tab=meaning_and_use#39620222">leer</a></span><span class="daterange">1586&ndash;1847</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition">To walk stealthily or with averted looks; to slink <i>away</i>. <i>Obsolete</i>.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/shab_v?tab=meaning_and_use#23089731">shab</a></span><span class="daterange">1699&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>intransitive</i> with <i>off</i> or <i>away</i>: To slink away, sneak off. Also, to fall away from one's engagement.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/slive_v2?tab=meaning_and_use#22270572">slive</a></span><span class="daterange">1707&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition">To slip <i>off</i> or away; to move quietly or slyly in some direction; to sneak or hang about; to loiter, idle.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/ghost_v?tab=meaning_and_use#3152431">ghost</a></span><span class="daterange">1833&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>intransitive</i>. To move or act in a manner characteristic or reminiscent of a ghost; (now) <i>spec.</i> to glide smoothly and effortlessly, esp. without&hellip;</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/coyote_v?tab=meaning_and_use#1403287110">coyote</a></span><span class="daterange">1855&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>intransitive</i>. <i>U.S.</i> <i>colloquial</i>. To move or go stealthily or furtively. Usually with adverbs, esp. in <b>to coyote out</b>: to escape, to extricate oneself.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/oil_v?tab=meaning_and_use#33397106">to oil out</a></span><span class="daterange">1945&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>intransitive</i>. <i>colloquial</i>. To move swiftly; to flit, fly. Also with <span class="cited-form">away</span>. Now&hellip;</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/rush_v2?tab=meaning_and_use#24528484">rush</a></span><span class="daterange">1852&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>intransitive</i>. To move briskly or with speed; <i>spec.</i> to move rapidly through the air with a sharp whining, buzzing, or ripping sound (see&hellip;</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/sail_v1?tab=meaning_and_use#24666236">sail</a></span><span class="daterange">1876&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition">Of persons, in various transferred senses. To move or go in a stately or dignified manner, suggestive of the movement of a ship under sail. (Chiefly&hellip;</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/rabbit_v2?tab=meaning_and_use#27217235">rabbit</a></span><span class="daterange">1887&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>intransitive</i>. <i>colloquial</i>. To move quickly or in the manner of a rabbit; to run away.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/shift_v?tab=meaning_and_use#22893856">shift</a></span><span class="daterange">1896&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>intransitive</i>. <i>colloquial</i> (chiefly <i>British</i>). To move quickly; to run quickly.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/giddy-up_v?tab=meaning_and_use#3165368">giddy-up</a></span><span class="daterange">1902&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>intransitive</i>. Esp. of a horse or rider: to move quickly; to get moving.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/move_v?tab=meaning_and_use#35811043">move</a></span><span class="daterange">1906&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>intransitive</i>. <i>colloquial</i>. To go quickly.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/high-tail_v?tab=meaning_and_use#1615826">high-tail</a></span><span class="daterange">1908&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>intransitive</i>, and <i>transitive</i> with <i>it</i> as object. To run (quickly) away; to move or leave quickly. Chiefly with adverbial complement.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/buzz_v1?tab=meaning_and_use#11393248">buzz</a></span><span class="daterange">1914&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>slang</i>. To go (quickly). <b>to buzz off</b>: to go off or away quickly. Also <b>to buzz in</b>: to come in (quickly), to enter.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/ball_v2?tab=meaning_and_use#29106315">to ball the jack</a></span><span class="daterange">1914&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>transitive</i>. <i>U.S.</i> <i>slang</i>. <b>to ball the jack</b>: to behave recklessly or wildly; (also) to travel fast, to hurry.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/burn_v1?tab=meaning_and_use#11864670">burn</a></span><span class="daterange">1942&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition">Of a motor car, etc.: to travel at speed. <i>slang</i>.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/hoosh_v?tab=meaning_and_use#1247387">hoosh</a></span><span class="daterange">1943&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>transitive</i>. To force or turn or drive (an animal, etc.) <i>off</i> (or <i>out</i>, etc.); also <i>intransitive</i>, to move (rapidly). Cf. also quot. 1943.</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/bomb_v?tab=meaning_and_use#16890616">bomb</a></span><span class="daterange">1958&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>intransitive</i>. <i>colloquial</i> (chiefly <i>British</i>). To move, travel, or drive fast. Frequently with adverb or prepositional phrase expressing direction. Cf. <i>l</i>&hellip;</div></div></li><li class="thesaurus-instance" data-sort-alpha="" data-sort-date=""><div class="thesaurus-instance-head"><span class="thesaurus-instance-lemma"><a href="/dictionary/shred_v?tab=meaning_and_use#23069282">shred</a></span><span class="daterange">1977&ndash;</span></div><div class="thesaurus-instance-body"> <div class="definition"><i>Surfing</i>. To cut or plough rapidly through (the water, etc.) on a surfboard; also <i>transferred</i>, to travel along (a track, etc.) at speed. Also <i>intrans</i>&hellip;</div></div></li></ol></div><div class="thesaurus-panel-link"><a class="call-to-action" href="/thesaurus/?classId=107531">View in Historical Thesaurus</a></div></div></div></div></div></li></ol></li><li class="item s2" id="4072791"><div class="item-content"><div class="item-enumerator">III.</div><div class="item-body"><div class="s2-header">Nautical uses.</div></div></div><ol class="list s4-list"><li class="item sense s4 " id="4072792"><div class="item-content"><div class="item-enumerator">III.12.</div><div class="item-body"><div class="daterange-container"><span class="daterange">1769&ndash;</span></div> <div class="definition"><span class="editorial-label">Nautical</span>. <span class="grammar">transitive</span>. To change the position of, shift (a block, rope, etc.). Also <span class="grammar">absol.</span>         [Substituted for the earlier <a class="cross-reference" href="/dictionary/flit_v?tab=meaning_and_use#4114959" data-tippy-content="Nautical. class="pronunciation-key-highlight">ˈ</mark> at the beginning of a syllable indicates that that syllable is pronounced with primary stress.</li><li>The symbol <mark class="pronunciation-key-highlight">ˌ</mark> at the beginning of a syllable indicates that that syllable is pronounced with secondary stress.</li><li>Round brackets <mark class="pronunciation-key-highlight">( )</mark> in a transcription indicate that the symbol within the brackets is optional.</li></ul><p>View the pronunciation model <a href="/information/understanding-entries/pronunciation/us-english-pronunciations/#model">here</a>.</p></section><p>&nbsp;</p></div></div><div class="pronunciation-key-group"><h4><button class="button pronunciation-key-button expandable" type="button" aria-controls="pronunciation-key-2" aria-expanded="false">Simple Text Respell</button></h4><div class="pronunciation-key hidden" id="pronunciation-key-2"><section class="pronunciation-key-ipa-section"><p>      Simple text respell breaks words into syllables, separated by a hyphen.      The syllable which carries the primary stress is written in capital letters.      This key covers both British and U.S. English Simple Text Respell.    </p><h5>Consonants</h5><p>b, d, f, h, k, l, m, n, p, r, s, t, v, w and z have their standard English values</p><ul     <h2 class="tab-header-label">Forms</h2>
</div><div class="tab-content"><div class="tab-content-body"><section id="variant-forms"><h3>Variant forms</h3><span class="grammar">infinitive</span> Old English  <span class="variant-form" id="4071633">fl&eacute;otan</span>, (<span class="grammar">3rd person present tense</span>.  <span class="variant-form" id="4071635">fl&yacute;t</span>), Middle English  <span class="variant-form" id="4071637">fleoten</span>, (Middle English  <span class="editorial-label">southern</span> <span class="variant-form" id="4071640">vleoten</span>,  <span class="variant-form" id="4071641">wleoten</span>), Middle English  <span class="variant-form" id="4071643">fleote</span>, Middle English&ndash;1500s  <span class="variant-form" id="4071645">flet(e(n</span>, Middle English&ndash;1600s  <span class="variant-form" id="4071647">fleete</span>, <span class="editorial-label">Scottish</span> <span class="variant-form" id="4071649">fleit</span>, Middle English&ndash;  <span class="variant-form" id="4071651">fleet</span>. <span class="grammar">past tense</span> Old English  <span class="variant-form" id="4071654">fl&eacute;at</span>, Middle English  <span class="work-title">Orm.</span> <span class="variant-form" id="4071657">fl&aelig;t</span>, Middle English  <span class="variant-form" id="4071659">fleet</span>,  <span class="variant-form" id="4071660">flote</span>, Middle English&ndash;1500s  <span class="variant-form" id="4071662">flet</span>, <span class="grammar">plural</span> Old English  <span class="variant-form" id="4071665">fluton</span>, Middle English  <span class="variant-form" id="4071667">fluten</span>,  <span class="variant-form" id="4071668">floten</span>; (1), Forms" data-reference-apa7="Fleet, v.&sup1;, Forms" data-version-date="December 2025">Cite</button></div></section></div></div></section><section class="tab-section hidden" id="frequency" role="tabpanel" tabindex="0" aria-labelledby="tab-control-frequency"><div class="tab-header invisible">
    <h2 class="tab-header-label">Frequency</h2>
</div><div class="tab-content"><div class="tab-content-body"><div id="frequency_text"><p><i>fleet</i> typically occurs about three times per million words in modern written English.</p><div class="frequency-caveat">      Frequency data is computed programmatically, and should be regarded as an estimate.    </div></div><div id="frequency_series"><section class="frequency-chart-container"><h3>Frequency of <i>fleet, v.&sup1;</i>, 1750&ndash;2010</h3><div><canvas id="frequency-chart-google_ngrams" class="frequency-chart" aria-describedby="frequency-table-google_ngrams"></canvas><p>          * Occurrences per million words in written English        </p></div><div class="frequency-explanation"><p>            Historical frequency series are derived from Google Books Ngrams (version 2),            a data set based on the Google Books corpus of several million books printed            in English between 1500 and 2010.          </p><p>            The overall frequency for a given word is calculated by summing frequencies            for the main form of the word, any plural or inflected forms, and any major            spelling variations.          </p><p>            For sets of homographs (distinct entries that share the same word-form,            e.g. <i>mole</i>, n.&sup1;, <i>mole</i>, n.&sup2;, <i>mole</i>, n.&sup3;, etc.), we have            estimated the frequency of each homograph entry as a fraction of the total            Ngrams frequency for the word-form. This may result in inaccuracies.           </p><p>      Smoothing has been applied to series for lower-frequency words, using      a moving-average algorithm. This reduces short-term fluctuations,      which may be produced by variability in the content of      the Google Books corpus.    </p></div><div class="frequency-table-section"><button class="frequency-table-expand-button expandable" type="button" aria-controls="frequency-table-google_ngrams" aria-expanded="false">Show data table</button><table id="frequency-table-google_ngrams"                         <p>Hello! I am the AI search assistant, here to help you use OED’s <b>advanced search tools</b>.
